> DEVELOPER

Implement the following plan:

# Plan: Remove classify_package and PackageKind

## Context

`classify_package()` hardcodes directory conventions (`apps/`, `libs/`, `tools/`, `*/tests`)
that are specific to one workspace layout. tspec shouldn't impose layout opinions — cargo
metadata already provides `has_binary`, and the only real behavioral need is excluding build
tools (tspec/xt/xtask) from batch operations.

## Changes

### `src/workspace.rs`

- **Remove** `PackageKind` enum (5 variants)
- **Remove** `classify_package()` function
- **Remove** `test_members()` method
- **Replace** `kind: PackageKind` with `is_build_tool: bool` on `PackageMember`
- **Add** `fn is_build_tool_name(name: &str) -> bool` — checks tspec/xt/xtask
- **Update** `discover()` — set `is_build_tool` instead of calling `classify_package`
- **Update** `buildable_members()` — filter `!m.is_build_tool`
- **Update** `runnable_members()` — filter `m.has_binary && !m.is_build_tool`
- **Replace unit tests** — drop all `classify_*` tests, add `is_build_tool_name` tests,
  update `discover_works` to check `is_build_tool` instead of `PackageKind`

### `src/all.rs`

- **Remove** `use crate::workspace::PackageKind` import
- **Remove** `use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt` import
- **Remove** `use crate::run::run_binary` import
- **Remove** `PackageKind::Test` skip in `test_all()` first loop
- **Remove** entire "Handle test packages" block in `test_all()` (test-binary-discovery)
- Result: `test_all()` just iterates `buildable_members()` and calls `test_package()` on each

### No changes needed

- `src/cmd/test.rs` — calls `buildable_members()` which keeps its signature
- `src/cmd/build.rs`, `run.rs`, `compare.rs` — only call `discover()` and filter methods

## Behavioral changes

- `runnable_members()` now returns any non-build-tool with a binary (was: only `App` kind)
- Test packages formerly getting special binary-discovery handling now get normal `cargo test`
- Neither change affects this repo — no `tools/` dir, no `-tests` packages

## Verification

tspec test -p tspec-build
tspec test --test integration_test -- --ignored
tspec install --path .
tspec test -p tspec
tspec clippy
tspec fmt --check
```

     1→//! Workspace discovery and package classification
     2→//!
     3→//! Uses `cargo metadata` to discover workspace members and classify them
     4→//! by type (app, lib, tool, test, build tool).
     5→
     6→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     7→use cargo_metadata::MetadataCommand;
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→/// Package classification for behavior differences
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
    12→pub enum PackageKind {
    13→    /// apps/* - application binaries, runnable
    14→    App,
    15→    /// libs/* - library crates
    16→    Lib,
    17→    /// tools/* - utility binaries
    18→    Tool,
    19→    /// */tests - test binary crates (special handling)
    20→    Test,
    21→    /// Build tools such as xtask or tspec, excluded by default
    22→    BuildTool,
    23→}
    24→
    25→/// Information about a workspace package
    26→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    27→pub struct PackageMember {
    28→    pub name: String,
    29→    pub path: PathBuf,
    30→    pub has_binary: bool,
    31→    pub kind: PackageKind,
    32→}
    33→
    34→/// Workspace information from cargo metadata
    35→pub struct WorkspaceInfo {
    36→    pub root: PathBuf,
    37→    pub members: Vec<PackageMember>,
    38→}
    39→
    40→impl WorkspaceInfo {
    41→    /// Workspace name derived from the root directory basename
    42→    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
    43→        self.root
    44→            .file_name()
    45→            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
    46→            .unwrap_or("workspace")
    47→    }
    48→
    49→    /// Discover workspace using cargo metadata
    50→    pub fn discover() -> Result<Self> {
    51→        let metadata = MetadataCommand::new()
    52→            .no_deps()
    53→            .exec()
    54→            .context("failed to run cargo metadata")?;
    55→
    56→        let root = metadata.workspace_root.as_std_path().to_path_buf();
    57→
    58→        let packages = metadata.workspace_packages();
    59→        let is_pop = packages.len() == 1;
    60→
    61→        let members: Vec<PackageMember> = packages
    62→            .iter()
    63→            .map(|pkg| {
    64→                let path = pkg
    65→                    .manifest_path
    66→                    .parent()
    67→                    .unwrap()
    68→                    .as_std_path()
    69→                    .to_path_buf();
    70→                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());
    71→                // POPs are always App — classify_package is for multi-package workspaces
    72→                let kind = if is_pop {
    73→                    PackageKind::App
    74→                } else {
    75→                    classify_package(&path, &pkg.name, &root, has_binary)
    76→                };
    77→
    78→                PackageMember {
    79→                    name: pkg.name.clone(),
    80→                    path,
    81→                    has_binary,
    82→                    kind,
    83→                }
    84→            })
    85→            .collect();
    86→
    87→        Ok(WorkspaceInfo { root, members })
    88→    }
    89→
    90→    /// Get members excluding build tools such as xtask or tspec
    91→    pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    92→        self.members
    93→            .iter()
    94→            .filter(|m| m.kind != PackageKind::BuildTool)
    95→            .collect()
    96→    }
    97→
    98→    /// Get members that can be run (apps only - have binaries and are runnable)
    99→    pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
   100→        self.members
   101→            .iter()
   102→            .filter(|m| m.has_binary && m.kind == PackageKind::App)
   103→            .collect()
   104→    }
   105→
   106→    /// Get test packages (special handling for rlibc-x2-tests etc.)
   107→    pub fn test_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
   108→        self.members
   109→            .iter()
   110→            .filter(|m| m.kind == PackageKind::Test)
   111→            .collect()
   112→    }
   113→}
   114→
   115→/// Classify a package based on its path relative to the workspace root.
   116→fn classify_package(
   117→    path: &Path,
   118→    name: &str,
   119→    workspace_root: &Path,
   120→    has_binary: bool,
   121→) -> PackageKind {
   122→    // Root package of a workspace is the main app
   123→    if path == workspace_root {
   124→        return PackageKind::App;
   125→    }
   126→
   127→    // Build tools at workspace root level
   128→    if name == "tspec" || name == "xt" || name == "xtask" {
   129→        return PackageKind::BuildTool;
   130→    }
   131→
   132→    // Use path relative to workspace root for classification
   133→    // (absolute paths can contain misleading segments like tests/fixtures/)
   134→    let rel_str = path
   135→        .strip_prefix(workspace_root)
   136→        .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
   137→        .unwrap_or_else(|_| path.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
   138→
   139→    // Test packages
   140→    if rel_str.contains("/tests") || rel_str.ends_with("tests") || name.ends_with("-tests") {
   141→        return PackageKind::Test;
   142→    }
   143→
   144→    // Categorize by directory
   145→    if rel_str.starts_with("apps/") {
   146→        PackageKind::App
   147→    } else if rel_str.starts_with("libs/") {
   148→        PackageKind::Lib
   149→    } else if rel_str.starts_with("tools/") {
   150→        PackageKind::Tool
   151→    } else if has_binary {
   152→        // Root-level members with binaries are apps
   153→        PackageKind::App
   154→    } else {
   155→        PackageKind::Lib
   156→    }
   157→}
   158→

     1→//! Batch operations on all workspace packages
     2→//!
     3→//! Provides build_all, run_all, test_all for operating on all workspace members.
     4→
     5→use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt;
     6→use std::process::ExitCode;
     7→
     8→use std::path::{Path, PathBuf};
     9→
    10→use crate::binary::{binary_size, strip_binary};
    11→use crate::cargo_build::{build_package, test_package};
    12→use crate::compare::{SpecResult, compare_specs, print_comparison};
    13→use crate::find_paths::find_tspecs;
    14→use crate::run::run_binary;
    15→use crate::tspec::spec_name_from_path;
    16→use crate::types::CargoFlags;
    17→use crate::workspace::{PackageKind, WorkspaceInfo};
    18→use crate::{print_header, print_hline};
    19→
    20→/// Normalize tspec patterns for per-package matching in all-packages mode.
    21→///
    22→/// Strips directory components so shell-expanded paths from the workspace root
    23→/// don't leak into sub-packages. Filters out non-tspec shell expansions
    24→/// (e.g. `target/`, `tools/`) — only `.ts.toml` files and glob patterns are kept.
    25→///
    26→/// Returns `None` if all patterns were filtered out (likely shell expansion of a
    27→/// glob that matched non-tspec entries). The caller should warn about quoting.
    28→fn normalize_tspec_patterns(patterns: &[String]) -> Option<Vec<String>> {
    29→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    30→        return Some(Vec::new());
    31→    }
    32→    let filenames: Vec<String> = patterns
    33→        .iter()
    34→        .filter_map(|p| {
    35→            let name = Path::new(p)
    36→                .file_name()
    37→                .map(|f| f.to_string_lossy().into_owned())
    38→                .unwrap_or_else(|| p.clone());
    39→            // Keep glob patterns (unexpanded) and actual tspec files
    40→            let is_glob = name.contains('*') || name.contains('?') || name.contains('[');
    41→            let is_tspec = name.ends_with(crate::TSPEC_SUFFIX);
    42→            if is_glob || is_tspec {
    43→                Some(name)
    44→            } else {
    45→                None
    46→            }
    47→        })
    48→        .collect();
    49→    if filenames.is_empty() && !patterns.is_empty() {
    50→        None // all patterns were filtered — likely shell expansion
    51→    } else {
    52→        Some(filenames)
    53→    }
    54→}
    55→
    56→/// Warn that shell glob expansion likely ate the tspec pattern.
    57→pub fn warn_shell_glob_expansion(patterns: &[String]) {
    58→    eprintln!(
    59→        "Warning: -t arguments ({}) don't look like tspec files.",
    60→        patterns.join(", ")
    61→    );
    62→    eprintln!("  The shell likely expanded your glob before tspec could see it.");
    63→    eprintln!("  Quote the pattern to prevent shell expansion: -t 'pattern*'");
    64→}
    65→
    66→/// Resolve tspec patterns for a workspace member.
    67→///
    68→/// Returns the matching spec paths, or an empty vec if no patterns match.
    69→/// When `patterns` is empty, returns an empty vec (caller should use default behavior).
    70→fn resolve_specs_for_member(member_path: &Path, patterns: &[String]) -> Vec<PathBuf> {
    71→    if patterns.is_empty() {
    72→        return Vec::new();
    73→    }
    74→    find_tspecs(member_path, patterns).unwrap_or_default()
    75→}
    76→
    77→/// Extract a short spec label from an optional tspec path.
    78→fn spec_label(tspec: &Option<String>) -> String {
    79→    match tspec {
    80→        Some(path) => spec_name_from_path(Path::new(path)),
    81→        None => String::new(),
    82→    }
    83→}
    84→
    85→/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
    86→pub struct OpResult {
    87→    pub name: String,
    88→    pub spec: String,
    89→    pub success: bool,
    90→    pub message: String,
    91→    pub size: Option<u64>,
    92→}
    93→
    94→/// Build all workspace packages (excluding build tools)
    95→///
    96→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
    97→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
    98→pub fn build_all(
    99→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   100→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   101→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   102→    strip: bool,
   103→    fail_fast: bool,
   104→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   105→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   106→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   107→        Some(n) => n,
   108→        None => {
   109→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   110→            return Vec::new();
   111→        }
   112→    };
   113→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   114→
   115→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   116→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   117→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   118→            continue;
   119→        }
   120→
   121→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   122→
   123→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   124→            vec![None]
   125→        } else {
   126→            specs
   127→                .into_iter()
   128→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   129→                .collect()
   130→        };
   131→
   132→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   133→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   134→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   135→                Ok(build_result) => {
   136→                    if strip
   137→                        && member.has_binary
   138→                        && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path)
   139→                    {
   140→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   141→                    }
   142→                    let size = binary_size(&build_result.binary_path).ok();
   143→                    OpResult {
   144→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   145→                        spec,
   146→                        success: true,
   147→                        message: format!("{}", build_result.binary_path.display()),
   148→                        size,
   149→                    }
   150→                }
   151→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   152→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   153→                    spec,
   154→                    success: false,
   155→                    message: e.to_string(),
   156→                    size: None,
   157→                },
   158→            };
   159→
   160→            let failed = !result.success;
   161→            results.push(result);
   162→
   163→            if failed && fail_fast {
   164→                return results;
   165→            }
   166→        }
   167→    }
   168→
   169→    results
   170→}
   171→
   172→/// Run all app packages sequentially
   173→///
   174→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   175→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   176→pub fn run_all(
   177→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   178→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   179→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   180→    strip: bool,
   181→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   182→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   183→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   184→        Some(n) => n,
   185→        None => {
   186→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   187→            return Vec::new();
   188→        }
   189→    };
   190→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   191→
   192→    for member in workspace.runnable_members() {
   193→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   194→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   195→            continue;
   196→        }
   197→
   198→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   199→
   200→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   201→            vec![None]
   202→        } else {
   203→            specs
   204→                .into_iter()
   205→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   206→                .collect()
   207→        };
   208→
   209→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   210→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   211→            let result = match build_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   212→                Ok(build_result) => {
   213→                    if strip && let Err(e) = strip_binary(&build_result.binary_path) {
   214→                        eprintln!("  warning: strip failed: {}", e);
   215→                    }
   216→                    match run_binary(&build_result.binary_path, &[]) {
   217→                        Ok(exit_code) => OpResult {
   218→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   219→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   220→                            success: true,
   221→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   222→                            size: None,
   223→                        },
   224→                        Err(e) => OpResult {
   225→                            name: member.name.clone(),
   226→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   227→                            success: false,
   228→                            message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   229→                            size: None,
   230→                        },
   231→                    }
   232→                }
   233→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   234→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   235→                    spec,
   236→                    success: false,
   237→                    message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   238→                    size: None,
   239→                },
   240→            };
   241→
   242→            results.push(result);
   243→        }
   244→    }
   245→
   246→    results
   247→}
   248→
   249→/// Test all workspace packages
   250→///
   251→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package uses its default spec.
   252→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   253→pub fn test_all(
   254→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   255→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   256→    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
   257→    fail_fast: bool,
   258→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   259→) -> Vec<OpResult> {
   260→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   261→        Some(n) => n,
   262→        None => {
   263→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   264→            return Vec::new();
   265→        }
   266→    };
   267→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   268→
   269→    // Test regular packages (excluding Test kind which needs special handling)
   270→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   271→        if member.kind == PackageKind::Test {
   272→            continue; // Handle test packages separately
   273→        }
   274→
   275→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   276→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   277→            continue;
   278→        }
   279→
   280→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   281→
   282→        let tspec_list: Vec<Option<String>> = if specs.is_empty() {
   283→            vec![None]
   284→        } else {
   285→            specs
   286→                .into_iter()
   287→                .map(|p| Some(p.to_string_lossy().into_owned()))
   288→                .collect()
   289→        };
   290→
   291→        for tspec in &tspec_list {
   292→            let spec = spec_label(tspec);
   293→            let result = match test_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   294→                Ok(()) => OpResult {
   295→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   296→                    spec,
   297→                    success: true,
   298→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   299→                    size: None,
   300→                },
   301→                Err(e) => OpResult {
   302→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   303→                    spec,
   304→                    success: false,
   305→                    message: e.to_string(),
   306→                    size: None,
   307→                },
   308→            };
   309→
   310→            let failed = !result.success;
   311→            results.push(result);
   312→
   313→            if failed && fail_fast {
   314→                return results;
   315→            }
   316→        }
   317→    }
   318→
   319→    // Handle test packages (like rlibc-x2-tests) - build and run all test binaries
   320→    for member in workspace.test_members() {
   321→        let specs = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   322→        if specs.is_empty() && !normalized.is_empty() {
   323→            continue;
   324→        }
   325→
   326→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   327→
   328→        // Build the test package (builds all binaries)
   329→        let build_tspec = specs.first().map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned());
   330→        let spec = spec_label(&build_tspec);
   331→        let build_result =
   332→            match build_package(&member.name, build_tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
   333→                Ok(r) => r,
   334→                Err(e) => {
   335→                    results.push(OpResult {
   336→                        name: member.name.clone(),
   337→                        spec,
   338→                        success: false,
   339→                        message: format!("build failed: {}", e),
   340→                        size: None,
   341→                    });
   342→                    if fail_fast {
   343→                        return results;
   344→                    }
   345→                    continue;
   346→                }
   347→            };
   348→
   349→        // Find and run all test binaries in the target directory
   350→        let profile_dir = crate::types::profile_dir_name(cli_profile.unwrap_or("debug"));
   351→        let target_dir = build_result.target_base.join(profile_dir);
   352→
   353→        // Look for binaries that end with "-tests" in the target directory
   354→        let test_binaries: Vec<_> = std::fs::read_dir(&target_dir)
   355→            .into_iter()
   356→            .flatten()
   357→            .filter_map(|e| e.ok())
   358→            .filter(|e| {
   359→                let name = e.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   360→                let is_file = e.path().is_file();
   361→                let is_test = name.ends_with("-tests");
   362→                let is_executable = e
   363→                    .metadata()
   364→                    .map(|m| m.permissions().mode() & 0o111 != 0)
   365→                    .unwrap_or(false);
   366→                is_file && is_test && is_executable
   367→            })
   368→            .collect();
   369→
   370→        if test_binaries.is_empty() {
   371→            results.push(OpResult {
   372→                name: member.name.clone(),
   373→                spec: spec.clone(),
   374→                success: false,
   375→                message: "no test binaries found".to_string(),
   376→                size: None,
   377→            });
   378→            continue;
   379→        }
   380→
   381→        for entry in test_binaries {
   382→            let path = entry.path();
   383→            let bin_name = entry.file_name().to_string_lossy().to_string();
   384→
   385→            print!("  Running {}... ", bin_name);
   386→
   387→            let result = match run_binary(&path, &[]) {
   388→                Ok(exit_code) => {
   389→                    if exit_code == 0 {
   390→                        println!("ok");
   391→                        OpResult {
   392→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   393→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   394→                            success: true,
   395→                            message: "ok".to_string(),
   396→                            size: None,
   397→                        }
   398→                    } else {
   399→                        println!("FAILED (exit {})", exit_code);
   400→                        OpResult {
   401→                            name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   402→                            spec: spec.clone(),
   403→                            success: false,
   404→                            message: format!("exit code: {}", exit_code),
   405→                            size: None,
   406→                        }
   407→                    }
   408→                }
   409→                Err(e) => {
   410→                    println!("FAILED");
   411→                    OpResult {
   412→                        name: format!("{}/{}", member.name, bin_name),
   413→                        spec: spec.clone(),
   414→                        success: false,
   415→                        message: format!("run failed: {}", e),
   416→                        size: None,
   417→                    }
   418→                }
   419→            };
   420→
   421→            let failed = !result.success;
   422→            results.push(result);
   423→
   424→            if failed && fail_fast {
   425→                return results;
   426→            }
   427→        }
   428→    }
   429→
   430→    results
   431→}
   432→
   433→/// A row for the summary table: package name, spec, and pre-formatted detail string.
   434→struct SummaryRow {
   435→    name: String,
   436→    spec: String,
   437→    detail: String,
   438→}
   439→
   440→/// Print a summary table with consistent formatting across all operations.
   441→///
   442→/// - `ws_name` — workspace/package name for the header
   443→/// - `cmd` — operation name ("TEST", "BUILD", "RUN", "COMPARE")
   444→/// - `detail_header` — column header for the detail column (e.g. "Status", "Status    Size", "Exit")
   445→/// - `rows` — pre-formatted rows
   446→/// - `footer` — footer line (e.g. "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed")
   447→fn print_summary_table(
   448→    ws_name: &str,
   449→    cmd: &str,
   450→    detail_header: &str,
   451→    rows: &[SummaryRow],
   452→    footer: &str,
   453→) {
   454→    let max_name_len = rows.iter().map(|r| r.name.len()).max().unwrap_or(7).max(7);
   455→    let has_spec = rows.iter().any(|r| !r.spec.is_empty());
   456→    let max_spec_len = if has_spec {
   457→        rows.iter().map(|r| r.spec.len()).max().unwrap_or(4).max(4)
   458→    } else {
   459→        0
   460→    };
   461→
   462→    println!();
   463→    print_header!(format!("{ws_name} {cmd} SUMMARY"));
   464→    if has_spec {
   465→        println!(
   466→            "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {detail_header}",
   467→            "Package",
   468→            "Spec",
   469→            nw = max_name_len,
   470→            sw = max_spec_len
   471→        );
   472→    } else {
   473→        println!(
   474→            "  {:width$}  {detail_header}",
   475→            "Package",
   476→            width = max_name_len
   477→        );
   478→    }
   479→
   480→    for row in rows {
   481→        if has_spec {
   482→            println!(
   483→                "  {:nw$}  {:sw$}  {}",
   484→                row.name,
   485→                row.spec,
   486→                row.detail,
   487→                nw = max_name_len,
   488→                sw = max_spec_len
   489→            );
   490→        } else {
   491→            println!(
   492→                "  {:width$}  {}",
   493→                row.name,
   494→                row.detail,
   495→                width = max_name_len
   496→            );
   497→        }
   498→    }
   499→
   500→    println!();
   501→    if !footer.is_empty() {
   502→        println!("  {footer}");
   503→    }
   504→    print_hline!();
   505→    println!();
   506→}
   507→
   508→/// Print a summary of operation results (for tests)
   509→pub fn print_test_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   510→    let mut passed = 0;
   511→    let mut failed = 0;
   512→
   513→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   514→        .iter()
   515→        .map(|r| {
   516→            let detail = if r.success {
   517→                passed += 1;
   518→                "[PASS]".to_string()
   519→            } else {
   520→                failed += 1;
   521→                "[FAIL]".to_string()
   522→            };
   523→            SummaryRow {
   524→                name: r.name.clone(),
   525→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   526→                detail,
   527→            }
   528→        })
   529→        .collect();
   530→
   531→    print_summary_table(
   532→        name,
   533→        "TEST",
   534→        "Status",
   535→        &rows,
   536→        &format!("Test: {passed} passed, {failed} failed"),
   537→    );
   538→
   539→    if failed > 0 {
   540→        ExitCode::from(1)
   541→    } else {
   542→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   543→    }
   544→}
   545→
   546→/// Print a summary for build operations (OK/FAILED)
   547→pub fn print_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   548→    let mut ok_count = 0;
   549→    let mut failed_count = 0;
   550→
   551→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   552→        .iter()
   553→        .map(|r| {
   554→            let status = if r.success {
   555→                ok_count += 1;
   556→                "[ OK ]"
   557→            } else {
   558→                failed_count += 1;
   559→                "[FAIL]"
   560→            };
   561→            let size_str = r.size.map(format_size).unwrap_or_else(|| "--".to_string());
   562→            SummaryRow {
   563→                name: r.name.clone(),
   564→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   565→                detail: format!("{status}  {size_str:>6}"),
   566→            }
   567→        })
   568→        .collect();
   569→
   570→    print_summary_table(
   571→        name,
   572→        "BUILD",
   573→        "Status    Size",
   574→        &rows,
   575→        &format!("Build: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   576→    );
   577→
   578→    if failed_count > 0 {
   579→        ExitCode::from(1)
   580→    } else {
   581→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   582→    }
   583→}
   584→
   585→fn format_size(bytes: u64) -> String {
   586→    if bytes >= 1_000_000 {
   587→        format!("{:.1}M", bytes as f64 / 1_000_000.0)
   588→    } else if bytes >= 1_000 {
   589→        format!("{:.1}K", bytes as f64 / 1_000.0)
   590→    } else {
   591→        format!("{}", bytes)
   592→    }
   593→}
   594→
   595→/// Result of a compare operation on a single package
   596→pub struct CompareResult {
   597→    pub op: OpResult,
   598→    pub specs: Vec<SpecResult>,
   599→}
   600→
   601→/// Compare all workspace packages that have binaries
   602→///
   603→/// When `tspec_patterns` is empty, each package discovers its own specs via default glob.
   604→/// When non-empty, patterns are resolved per-package; packages with no matches are skipped.
   605→pub fn compare_all(
   606→    workspace: &WorkspaceInfo,
   607→    tspec_patterns: &[String],
   608→    fail_fast: bool,
   609→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   610→) -> Vec<CompareResult> {
   611→    let normalized = match normalize_tspec_patterns(tspec_patterns) {
   612→        Some(n) => n,
   613→        None => {
   614→            warn_shell_glob_expansion(tspec_patterns);
   615→            return Vec::new();
   616→        }
   617→    };
   618→    let mut results = Vec::new();
   619→
   620→    for member in workspace.buildable_members() {
   621→        if !member.has_binary {
   622→            continue;
   623→        }
   624→
   625→        let spec_paths = if normalized.is_empty() {
   626→            find_tspecs(&member.path, &[]).unwrap_or_default()
   627→        } else {
   628→            let resolved = resolve_specs_for_member(&member.path, &normalized);
   629→            if resolved.is_empty() {
   630→                continue;
   631→            }
   632→            resolved
   633→        };
   634→
   635→        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   636→
   637→        let (op, specs) = match compare_specs(&member.name, &spec_paths, flags) {
   638→            Ok(spec_results) => (
   639→                OpResult {
   640→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   641→                    spec: String::new(),
   642→                    success: true,
   643→                    message: "ok".to_string(),
   644→                    size: None,
   645→                },
   646→                spec_results,
   647→            ),
   648→            Err(e) => (
   649→                OpResult {
   650→                    name: member.name.clone(),
   651→                    spec: String::new(),
   652→                    success: false,
   653→                    message: e.to_string(),
   654→                    size: None,
   655→                },
   656→                Vec::new(),
   657→            ),
   658→        };
   659→
   660→        let failed = !op.success;
   661→        results.push(CompareResult { op, specs });
   662→
   663→        if failed && fail_fast {
   664→            break;
   665→        }
   666→    }
   667→
   668→    results
   669→}
   670→
   671→/// Print a summary for compare operations
   672→///
   673→/// With a single package, just prints its comparison table.
   674→/// With multiple packages, reprints all per-package tables then an overall OK/FAIL summary.
   675→pub fn print_compare_summary(name: &str, results: &[CompareResult]) -> ExitCode {
   676→    let has_failure = results.iter().any(|r| !r.op.success);
   677→
   678→    // Reprint per-package comparison tables together
   679→    for result in results {
   680→        if !result.specs.is_empty() {
   681→            print_comparison(&result.op.name, &result.specs);
   682→        }
   683→    }
   684→
   685→    // Only show overall COMPARE SUMMARY when there are multiple packages
   686→    if results.len() > 1 {
   687→        let mut ok_count = 0;
   688→        let mut failed_count = 0;
   689→
   690→        let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   691→            .iter()
   692→            .map(|r| {
   693→                let detail = if r.op.success {
   694→                    ok_count += 1;
   695→                    "[ OK ]".to_string()
   696→                } else {
   697→                    failed_count += 1;
   698→                    "[FAIL]".to_string()
   699→                };
   700→                SummaryRow {
   701→                    name: r.op.name.clone(),
   702→                    spec: r.op.spec.clone(),
   703→                    detail,
   704→                }
   705→            })
   706→            .collect();
   707→
   708→        print_summary_table(
   709→            name,
   710→            "COMPARE",
   711→            "Status",
   712→            &rows,
   713→            &format!("Compare: {ok_count} ok, {failed_count} failed"),
   714→        );
   715→    }
   716→
   717→    if has_failure {
   718→        ExitCode::from(1)
   719→    } else {
   720→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   721→    }
   722→}
   723→
   724→/// Print a summary for run operations (shows exit codes, not pass/fail)
   725→pub fn print_run_summary(name: &str, results: &[OpResult]) -> ExitCode {
   726→    let mut error_count = 0;
   727→
   728→    let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
   729→        .iter()
   730→        .map(|r| {
   731→            let detail = if r.success {
   732→                let code = r.message.strip_prefix("exit code: ").unwrap_or(&r.message);
   733→                format!("{code:>4}")
   734→            } else {
   735→                error_count += 1;
   736→                format!("ERROR: {}", r.message)
   737→            };
   738→            SummaryRow {
   739→                name: r.name.clone(),
   740→                spec: r.spec.clone(),
   741→                detail,
   742→            }
   743→        })
   744→        .collect();
   745→
   746→    let footer = if error_count > 0 {
   747→        format!("Run: {error_count} error(s)")
   748→    } else {
   749→        String::new()
   750→    };
   751→
   752→    print_summary_table(name, "RUN", "Exit", &rows, &footer);
   753→
   754→    if error_count > 0 {
   755→        ExitCode::from(1)
   756→    } else {
   757→        ExitCode::SUCCESS
   758→    }
   759→}
   760→
```

name == \"tspec\" || name == \"xt\" || name == \"xtask\" {\n        return PackageKind::BuildTool;\n    }\n\n    // Use path relative to workspace root for classification\n    // (absolute paths can contain misleading segments like tests/fixtures/)\n    let rel_str = path\n        .strip_prefix(workspace_root)\n        .map(|p| p.to_string_lossy().into_owned())\n        .unwrap_or_else(|_| assert!(!info.members.is_empty());\n\n            let buildable = info.buildable_members();\n            if info.members.len() == 1 {\n                // POP: the single package should be buildable\n   "new_string": "/// Check if a package name is a build tool (excluded from batch operations).\nfn is_build_tool_name(name: &str) -> bool {\n    matches!(name, \"tspec\" | \"xt\" | \"xtask\")\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn build_tool_names() {\n        assert!(is_build_tool_name(\"tspec\"));\n        assert!(is_build_tool_name(\"xt\"));\n        assert!(is_build_tool_name(\"xtask\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn non_build_tool_names() {\n        assert!(!is_build_tool_name(\"tspec-build\"));\n        assert!(!is_build_tool_name(\"my-app\"));\n        assert!(!is_build_tool_name(\"xtask-macros\"));\n        assert!(!is_build_tool_name(\"foo-tests\"));\n    }\n\n    #[test]\n    fn discover_works() {\n        // This test works for both workspaces and POPs\n        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover();\n        if let Ok(info) = info {\n            assert!(!info.members.is_empty());\n\n            let buildable = info.buildable_members();\n            if info.members.len() == 1 {\n                // POP: the single package should be buildable\n                assert_eq!(buildable.len(), 1);\n            } else {\n                // Workspace: build tools should be excluded from buildable\n                assert!(\n                    buildable\n                        .iter()\n                        .all(|m| !m.is_build_tool)\n                );\n            }\n\n            let _runnable = info.runnable_members();\n            // Just verify it doesn't panic - count depends on project structure\n        }\n        // If cargo metadata fails (e.g., not in a Rust project), that's OK for CI\n    }\n}"
}

> AGENT

Done. Here's a summary of the changes:

**`src/workspace.rs`:**
- Removed `PackageKind` enum (5 variants) and `classify_package()` function
- Removed `test_members()` method
- Replaced `kind: PackageKind` with `is_build_tool: bool` on `PackageMember`
- Added `is_build_tool_name()` — checks `tspec`/`xt`/`xtask`
- Updated `discover()` to set `is_build_tool` directly
- Updated `buildable_members()` to filter `!m.is_build_tool`
- Updated `runnable_members()` to filter `m.has_binary && !m.is_build_tool` (was: only `App` kind)
- Replaced all 11 `classify_*` tests with 2 focused `is_build_tool_name` tests, updated `discover_works`
- Removed unused `use std::path::Path`

**`src/all.rs`:**
- Removed `use crate::workspace::PackageKind` import
- Removed `use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt` import
- Removed `PackageKind::Test` skip in `test_all()`
- Removed entire 100-line "Handle test packages" block (test-binary-discovery)
- `test_all()` now simply iterates `buildable_members()` and calls `test_package()` on each

**Verification:** 265 unit tests, 10 tspec-build tests, 16 integration tests — all passing. Clippy and fmt clean.

     3→## 20260219 - Test infrastructure: fixture workspaces and test args

    10→Three project structures need test coverage:

{
  "replace_all": false,

  "old_string": "# Chores 6",
  "new_string": "# Chores 6\n\n## 20260220 - Remove classify_package and PackageKind\n\n### Context\n\n`classify_package()` hardcoded directory conventions (`apps/`, `libs/`,\n`tools/`, `*/tests`) specific to one workspace layout. tspec shouldn't\nimpose layout opinions — cargo metadata already provides `has_binary`,\nand the only real behavioral need is excluding build tools (tspec/xt/xtask)\nfrom batch operations.\n\n### Changes\n\n**`src/workspace.rs`:**\n- Removed `PackageKind` enum (5 variants) and `classify_package()` fn\n- Removed `test_members()` method\n- Replaced `kind: PackageKind` with `is_build_tool: bool` on `PackageMember`\n- Added `is_build_tool_name()` — checks tspec/xt/xtask\n- Updated `discover()`, `buildable_members()`, `runnable_members()`\n- Replaced 11 classify tests with 2 `is_build_tool_name` tests\n\n**`src/all.rs`:**\n- Removed `PackageKind`, `PermissionsExt` imports\n- Removed `PackageKind::Test` skip and entire 100-line\n  test-binary-discovery block from `test_all()`\n- `test_all()` now just iterates `buildable_members()` + `test_package()`\n\n### Behavioral changes\n\n- `runnable_members()` returns any non-build-tool with a binary\n  (was: only `App` kind)\n- Test packages formerly getting special binary-discovery handling\n  now get normal `cargo test`\n- Neither change affects this repo"
}
```

wink@3900x 26-02-20T23:22:13.383Z:~/data/prgs/rust/tspec (main)

> AGENT

That's the `is_build_tool` change — `tspec` is now excluded from `buildable_members()` because `is_build_tool_name("tspec")` returns `true`. So `tspec test` (all-packages mode) skips `tspec` itself and only tests `tspec-build`.

This is correct behavior for other workspaces (you don't want `tspec test` to test the build tool in a user's project), but it's awkward here since tspec's own repo needs to test itself.

The workaround is what we've been doing: `tspec test -p tspec` explicitly names the package and bypasses the `buildable_members()` filter. But I agree it's surprising that `tspec test` at the workspace root doesn't include tspec.

Want me to look into this? One option would be to only exclude build tools when running in a foreign workspace (not the tspec repo itself), but that feels hacky. Another would be to only exclude `xt`/`xtask` and not `tspec` from the build tool list — the rationale being that `tspec` is a real package users might depend on, while `xt`/`xtask` are always internal scaffolding.

> DEVELOPER

xt/xtask are vistigial when the precuror of tspec was in rlibc-x

> AGENT

Good point — `xt`/`xtask` were only relevant in the old rlibc-x workspace. There's no reason to hardcode exclusions for them in tspec. The only build tool tspec should know about is itself, and as we just saw, excluding itself is counterproductive.

So `is_build_tool_name` should just be removed entirely, and `is_build_tool` should always be `false`. Or simpler: just remove `is_build_tool` altogether and have `buildable_members()` and `runnable_members()` return all members.

Want me to go ahead and remove `is_build_tool` completely?

     1→//! Workspace discovery
     2→//!
     3→//! Uses `cargo metadata` to discover workspace members and determine
     4→//! which are build tools (excluded from batch operations by default).
     5→
     6→use anyhow::{Context, Result};
     7→use cargo_metadata::MetadataCommand;
     8→use std::path::PathBuf;
     9→
    10→/// Information about a workspace package
    11→#[derive(Debug, Clone)]
    12→pub struct PackageMember {
    13→    pub name: String,
    14→    pub path: PathBuf,
    15→    pub has_binary: bool,
    16→    pub is_build_tool: bool,
    17→}
    18→
    19→/// Workspace information from cargo metadata
    20→pub struct WorkspaceInfo {
    21→    pub root: PathBuf,
    22→    pub members: Vec<PackageMember>,
    23→}
    24→
    25→impl WorkspaceInfo {
    26→    /// Workspace name derived from the root directory basename
    27→    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {
    28→        self.root
    29→            .file_name()
    30→            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())
    31→            .unwrap_or("workspace")
    32→    }
    33→
    34→    /// Discover workspace using cargo metadata
    35→    pub fn discover() -> Result<Self> {
    36→        let metadata = MetadataCommand::new()
    37→            .no_deps()
    38→            .exec()
    39→            .context("failed to run cargo metadata")?;
    40→
    41→        let root = metadata.workspace_root.as_std_path().to_path_buf();
    42→
    43→        let members: Vec<PackageMember> = metadata
    44→            .workspace_packages()
    45→            .iter()
    46→            .map(|pkg| {
    47→                let path = pkg
    48→                    .manifest_path
    49→                    .parent()
    50→                    .unwrap()
    51→                    .as_std_path()
    52→                    .to_path_buf();
    53→                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());
    54→
    55→                PackageMember {
    56→                    name: pkg.name.clone(),
    57→                    path,
    58→                    has_binary,
    59→                    is_build_tool: is_build_tool_name(&pkg.name),
    60→                }
    61→            })
    62→            .collect();
    63→
    64→        Ok(WorkspaceInfo { root, members })
    65→    }
    66→
    67→    /// Get members excluding build tools such as xtask or tspec
    68→    pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    69→        self.members.iter().filter(|m| !m.is_build_tool).collect()
    70→    }
    71→
    72→    /// Get members that can be run (have binaries and are not build tools)
    73→    pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {
    74→        self.members
    75→            .iter()
    76→            .filter(|m| m.has_binary && !m.is_build_tool)
    77→            .collect()
    78→    }
    79→}
    80→
    81→/// Check if a package name is a build tool (excluded from batch operations).
    82→fn is_build_tool_name(name: &str) -> bool {
    83→    matches!(name, "tspec" | "xt" | "xtask")
    84→}
    85→
    86→#[cfg(test)]
    87→mod tests {
    88→    use super::*;
    89→
    90→    #[test]
    91→    fn build_tool_names() {
    92→        assert!(is_build_tool_name("tspec"));
    93→        assert!(is_build_tool_name("xt"));
    94→        assert!(is_build_tool_name("xtask"));
    95→    }
    96→
    97→    #[test]
    98→    fn non_build_tool_names() {
    99→        assert!(!is_build_tool_name("tspec-build"));
   100→        assert!(!is_build_tool_name("my-app"));
   101→        assert!(!is_build_tool_name("xtask-macros"));
   102→        assert!(!is_build_tool_name("foo-tests"));
   103→    }
   104→
   105→    #[test]
   106→    fn discover_works() {
   107→        // This test works for both workspaces and POPs
   108→        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover();
   109→        if let Ok(info) = info {
   110→            assert!(!info.members.is_empty());
   111→
   112→            let buildable = info.buildable_members();
   113→            if info.members.len() == 1 {
   114→                // POP: the single package should be buildable
   115→                assert_eq!(buildable.len(), 1);
   116→            } else {
   117→                // Workspace: build tools should be excluded from buildable
   118→                assert!(buildable.iter().all(|m| !m.is_build_tool));
   119→            }
   120→
   121→            let _runnable = info.runnable_members();
   122→            // Just verify it doesn't panic - count depends on project structure
   123→        }
   124→        // If cargo metadata fails (e.g., not in a Rust project), that's OK for CI
   125→    }
   126→}

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/workspace.rs",
  "content": "//! Workspace discovery\n//!\n//! Uses `cargo metadata` to discover workspace members.\n\nuse anyhow::{Context, Result};\nuse cargo_metadata::MetadataCommand;\nuse std::path::PathBuf;\n\n/// Information about a workspace package\n#[derive(Debug, Clone)]\npub struct PackageMember {\n    pub name: String,\n    pub path: PathBuf,\n    pub has_binary: bool,\n}\n\n/// Workspace information from cargo metadata\npub struct WorkspaceInfo {\n    pub root: PathBuf,\n    pub members: Vec<PackageMember>,\n}\n\nimpl WorkspaceInfo {\n    /// Workspace name derived from the root directory basename\n    pub fn name(&self) -> &str {\n        self.root\n            .file_name()\n            .and_then(|n| n.to_str())\n            .unwrap_or(\"workspace\")\n    }\n\n    /// Discover workspace using cargo metadata\n    pub fn discover() -> Result<Self> {\n        let metadata = MetadataCommand::new()\n            .no_deps()\n            .exec()\n            .context(\"failed to run cargo metadata\")?;\n\n        let root = metadata.workspace_root.as_std_path().to_path_buf();\n\n        let members: Vec<PackageMember> = metadata\n            .workspace_packages()\n            .iter()\n            .map(|pkg| {\n                let path = pkg\n                    .manifest_path\n                    .parent()\n                    .unwrap()\n                    .as_std_path()\n                    .to_path_buf();\n                let has_binary = pkg.targets.iter().any(|t| t.is_bin());\n\n                PackageMember {\n                    name: pkg.name.clone(),\n                    path,\n                    has_binary,\n                }\n            })\n            .collect();\n\n        Ok(WorkspaceInfo { root, members })\n    }\n\n    /// Get all workspace members\n    pub fn buildable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {\n        self.members.iter().collect()\n    }\n\n    /// Get members that have binaries\n    pub fn runnable_members(&self) -> Vec<&PackageMember> {\n        self.members.iter().filter(|m| m.has_binary).collect()\n    }\n}\n\n#[cfg(test)]\nmod tests {\n    use super::*;\n\n    #[test]\n    fn discover_works() {\n        // This test works for both workspaces and POPs\n        let info = WorkspaceInfo::discover();\n        if let Ok(info) = info {\n            assert!(!info.members.is_empty());\n\n            // All members should be buildable\n            assert_eq!(info.buildable_members().len(), info.members.len());\n\n            let _runnable = info.runnable_members();\n            // Just verify it doesn't panic - count depends on project structure\n        }\n        // If cargo metadata fails (e.g., not in a Rust project), that's OK for CI\n    }\n}\n"
}

> AGENT

Both packages now appear in the summary:

```
  Package      Status
  tspec-build  [PASS]
  tspec        [PASS]

  Test: 2 passed, 0 failed
```

> DEVELOPER

working but I want integration tests run and in this case they are instant so time isn't a rationle, IMO. Appending `-- --ignored` is weird and unexpected. Convince me it's deserialbe

> AGENT

You're right that for this repo the integration tests are fast. But `#[ignore]` isn't about time here — it's about **environment requirements**. These tests call `tspec build` and `cargo build` on fixture packages, which means they need:

- A working Rust toolchain
- `tspec` installed on PATH
- Network access (first build pulls crates)

Unit tests have zero external dependencies — they run in any CI container, any sandbox, any `cargo test` invocation. The `#[ignore]` boundary keeps that guarantee clean.

That said, your counterarguments are strong:

1. **Anyone running `tspec test` already has all those dependencies** — they're developing tspec
2. **`-- --ignored` is cargo's convention, not a good UX** — nobody discovers it naturally
3. **Fast tests that don't run are worthless tests** — the whole point of integration tests is catching regressions

I think the right move is to remove `#[ignore]` from all 16 tests. Anyone running `tspec test` is a tspec developer with the full environment. Want me to do that?

> DEVELOPER

yes remove #[ignore]

    24→
    25→    assert_eq!(spec.panic, Some(PanicMode::Abort));
    26→    assert_eq!(spec.cargo.profile, Some("release".to_string()));
    27→}
    28→

    38→        .expect("failed to run cargo build");
    39→
    40→    assert!(

     1→# Chores 6
     2→
     3→## 20260220 - Remove classify_package and PackageKind
     4→
     5→### Context
     6→
     7→`classify_package()` hardcoded directory conventions (`apps/`, `libs/`,
     8→`tools/`, `*/tests`) specific to one workspace layout. tspec shouldn't
     9→impose layout opinions — cargo metadata already provides `has_binary`,
    10→and the only real behavioral need is excluding build tools (tspec/xt/xtask)
    11→from batch operations.
    12→
    13→### Changes
    14→
    15→**`src/workspace.rs`:**
    16→- Removed `PackageKind` enum (5 variants) and `classify_package()` fn
    17→- Removed `test_members()` method
    18→- Replaced `kind: PackageKind` with `is_build_tool: bool` on `PackageMember`
    19→- Added `is_build_tool_name()` — checks tspec/xt/xtask
    20→- Updated `discover()`, `buildable_members()`, `runnable_members()`
    21→- Replaced 11 classify tests with 2 `is_build_tool_name` tests
    22→
    23→**`src/all.rs`:**
    24→- Removed `PackageKind`, `PermissionsExt` imports
    25→- Removed `PackageKind::Test` skip and entire 100-line
    26→  test-binary-discovery block from `test_all()`
    27→- `test_all()` now just iterates `buildable_members()` + `test_package()`
    28→
    29→### Behavioral changes
    30→
    31→- `runnable_members()` returns any non-build-tool with a binary
    32→  (was: only `App` kind)
    33→- Test packages formerly getting special binary-discovery handling
    34→  now get normal `cargo test`
    35→- Neither change affects this repo
    36→
    37→## 20260219 - Test infrastructure: fixture workspaces and test args
    38→
    39→### Context
    40→
    41→tspec has 255 unit tests but zero integration tests that actually build real packages.
    42→The top todo item is "Add a permanent test workspace for integration testing."
    43→
    44→Three project structures need test coverage:
    45→1. **POP** — Plain Old Package (single Cargo.toml, no workspace)
    46→2. **POP+WS** — POP with embedded workspace (like tspec itself)
    47→3. **POWS** — Pure Old Workspace (workspace root, no package)
    48→
    49→### Plan
    50→
    51→**dev1:** Add test selection flags to `tspec test`:
    52→- `--test <TARGET>` (repeatable) — select specific test targets
    53→- `--names/-n <NAME>...` — filter tests by name (OR-matched substrings via test harness)
    54→- `--list/-l` — list test targets and functions (grouped with counts)
    55→- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing args to test binary (--ignored, --exact, etc.)
    56→All flow through `CargoFlags.extra_args` — no new parameters to `test_package()`/`run_cargo()`.
    57→
    58→**dev2:** POP fixture in `tests/fixtures/pop/` with copy-to-tmpdir helper and `#[ignore]` integration tests.
    59→
    60→**dev3:** POP+WS fixture in `tests/fixtures/pop-ws/` with workspace discovery and cross-package tests.
    61→
    62→**dev4:** POWS fixture in `tests/fixtures/pows/` with all-packages mode tests.
    63→
    64→### Status (20260220)
    65→
    66→**Branch:** `feat-test-fixtures` off `main` (at `a98ebad`)
    67→
    68→**Completed:**
    69→- dev0: `534ce4b` — marker commit, documented -dev0 convention in CLAUDE.md + README.md
    70→- dev1: `28baac3` — test selection flags (--test, --names, --list, --target-names, trailing args)
    71→  - Also added: `extra_args: Vec<String>` to `CargoFlags` in types.rs
    72→  - Also added: workspace-mode --test auto-filters to packages that have the target
    73→  - 268 unit tests, all passing. Installed and verified.
    74→- dev2: `f85a1cb` — POP fixture + integration tests
    75→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pop/` with Cargo.toml, src/main.rs, tspec.ts.toml
    76→  - Added `exclude = ["tests/fixtures"]` to root Cargo.toml `[workspace]`
    77→  - Created `tests/fixture.rs` helper: `copy_fixture(name) -> (TempDir, PathBuf)`
    78→  - Created `tests/integration_test.rs` with 4 `#[ignore]` tests
    79→  - Bug fix: `find_project_root()` now respects workspace `exclude` list
    80→  - Added `is_excluded_from_workspace()` function + 4 unit tests
    81→- dev3: `5c24d4b` — POP+WS fixture
    82→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pop-ws/` with root binary + mylib member
    83→  - Added 6 integration tests (spec load, cargo build, tspec build root/member/all, build from member dir)
    84→  - Bug fix: `classify_package()` now uses paths relative to workspace root
    85→  - Added unit test for path-under-tests-dir classification
    86→- dev4: POWS fixture
    87→  - Created `tests/fixtures/pows/` with workspace-only root (no [package]), pows-app binary, mylib library
    88→  - Added 6 integration tests (cargo build, tspec build all/member/from-member-dir, tspec test all, dot-resolves-to-all)
    89→  - Fix: directory name must match package name for `find_package_dir()` resolution (pows-app/ not app/)
    90→  - All 16 integration tests passing, 268 unit tests passing
    91→
    92→**Remaining:** release (v0.15.0)
    93→
    94→### Future: --manifest-path / --path flag
    95→
    96→Add a way to run tspec against a project without cd'ing into it. Cargo uses `--manifest-path <path/to/Cargo.toml>` (not `--path` — unclear why). We could follow cargo's convention (`--manifest-path`) or use `--path <dir>` for convenience. Would simplify integration tests and general usability. Not blocking dev2-dev4.
    97→
    98→### Documentation TODO
    99→
   100→Where and how to document the test fixtures (decide before release):
   101→- `README.md` — new `## Testing tspec` section documenting when/how to run integration tests
   102→- `tests/README.md` — purpose of the tests directory, overview of unit vs integration tests
   103→- `tests/fixtures/README.md` — purpose of fixtures, how copy_fixture() works
   104→- `tests/fixtures/pop/README.md` (and each fixture) — purpose of that specific fixture
   105→
   106→Open question: should the per-fixture READMEs be the "main" docs that README.md links to, or should README.md be self-contained with the READMEs as secondary?
   107→
   108→Full plan: `.claude/plans/enumerated-booping-curry.md`
   109→

  "new_string": "### Changes\n\n**`src/workspace.rs`:**\n- Removed `PackageKind` enum (5 variants) and `classify_package()` fn\n- Removed `test_members()` method and `is_build_tool` field\n- `PackageMember` now has only `name`, `path`, `has_binary`\n- `buildable_members()` returns all members (no exclusions)\n- `runnable_members()` returns all members with binaries\n- Replaced 11 classify tests with 1 `discover_works` test\n\n**`src/all.rs`:**\n- Removed `PackageKind`, `PermissionsExt` imports\n- Removed `PackageKind::Test` skip and entire 100-line\n  test-binary-discovery block from `test_all()`\n- `test_all()` now just iterates `buildable_members()` + `test_package()`\n\n**`tests/integration_test.rs`:**\n- Removed `#[ignore]` from all 16 integration tests — they run\n  by default now since they're fast and anyone running `tspec test`\n  has the full toolchain\n\n### Rationale for removing is_build_tool\n\nThe `xt`/`xtask` exclusions were vestigial from the rlibc-x workspace.\nExcluding `tspec` from its own `tspec test` was counterproductive —\n`tspec test` only showed tspec-build in the summary. No workspace\nlayout opinions should be baked into tspec.\n\n### Behavioral changes\n\n- `tspec test` (all-packages mode) now includes all workspace members\n- `runnable_members()` returns any member with a binary (no exclusions)\n- Integration tests run by default (no `-- --ignored` needed)"

   299→# Pass args to the test harness (after --)
   300→tspec test -- --ignored                    # Run only #[ignore] tests
   301→tspec test --test integration_test -- --ignored --nocapture  # Combined
   302→```
   303→
   304→**Test flags:**
   305→- `-n, --names <FILTER>...` — match anywhere in the qualified test name (e.g., `-n remove` matches `ts_cmd::remove::tests::remove_by_index`; multiple filters are OR-matched)
   306→- `--test <TARGET-NAME>` — run only the named test target (the basename of a file in `tests/` without `.rs`; repeatable). In workspace mode, only packages containing the target are tested. Use `--target-names` to see available names
   307→- `--target-names` — list available target names for `--test`
   308→- `--list` / `-l` — list all test functions grouped by target
   309→- `-- <ARGS>` — pass trailing arguments to the test binary (e.g., `--ignored`, `--exact`, `--nocapture`)
   310→
   311→## Project Structure
   312→
   313→```
   314→tspec/                  # Workspace root (also the main tspec package)
   315→  Cargo.toml            # [workspace] + [package] — workspace with root package
   316→  src/
   317→    lib.rs              # Library root, exposes modules
```

> DEVELOPER

hmm, that example `tspec test -n pop -- --exact` looks like it results in no tests running yet it passes, really weird. I think if the number of tests run is 0 we should indicate that. It'd be nice if the number included the number of individual tests succeeded and failed, thoughts?

> AGENT

Good catches on both.

The example is just bad — `--exact` with a substring like "pop" matches nothing, and cargo considers 0 tests run as success. Let me fix the example first, then address the feature ideas.

For the feature ideas:
1. **Warn/fail on 0 tests** — makes sense. If cargo reports 0 tests ran, that's almost always a mistake (typo in filter, wrong `--test` target). We could parse cargo's output for the `running N tests` / `test result:` lines and flag it.
2. **Show individual test counts in summary** — would require parsing cargo's test result lines (e.g., `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed`) and aggregating them. The summary would look something like:

```
  Package      Status  Tests
  tspec-build  [PASS]  10 passed
  tspec        [PASS]  279 passed

  Test: 2 packages, 289 passed, 0 failed
```

Both are real improvements but separate work from the current refactor. Want me to fix the README example now and add these as todo items?

wink@3900x 26-02-20T23:53:29.460Z:~/data/prgs/rust/tspec (refactor-remove-clasify-package)

Testing tspec-build with spec /home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/tspec-build/tspec.ts.toml

    tspec-build/src/lib.rs - emit_linker_flags_from (line 28)

test result: ok. 2 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.02s

> AGENT

That's cargo's own output, not a tspec bug. When you pass `--show-output`, cargo's test harness prints a `successes:` section for captured stdout — even when there's nothing to show, which gives the empty double `successes:`. You can reproduce it with plain `cargo test -- --show-output` too.

Not something tspec can fix (it's passthrough). But it does make `--show-output` a poor README example since it looks broken. Want me to pick a different example, or leave it since it's technically correct?

> DEVELOPER

Actually the `-- --xxx` is weird and how does the user know what it's being passed to, they don't have the source and I don't know what is actually happening!

> AGENT

You're right — the `-- <ARGS>` feature is cargo's convention and it's opaque. The args get passed to the test binary (Rust's built-in test harness), but there's no way for a user to know that without reading cargo docs. And tspec's job is to be simpler than cargo, not to expose cargo's quirks.

Let me just drop that example entirely and keep the section focused on tspec's own flags:

> TOOL

+- Removed `#[ignore]` from all 16 integration tests — they run
+  by default now since they're fast and anyone running `tspec test`
+  has the full toolchain
+
+### Rationale for removing is_build_tool
+
+The `xt`/`xtask` exclusions were vestigial from the rlibc-x workspace.
+Excluding `tspec` from its own `tspec test` was counterproductive —
+`tspec test` only showed tspec-build in the summary. No workspace
+layout opinions should be baked into tspec.
+
 ### Behavioral changes
 
-- `runnable_members()` returns any non-build-tool with a binary
-  (was: only `App` kind)
-- Test packages formerly getting special binary-discovery handling
-  now get normal `cargo test`
-- Neither change affects this repo
+- `tspec test` (all-packages mode) now includes all workspace members
+- `runnable_members()` returns any member with a binary (no exclusions)
+- Integration tests run by default (no `-- --ignored` needed)

```
Excellent! Now I have a comprehensive understanding of the test flow. Let me create a detailed report:

## Report: How tspec Runs Tests and Handles Output

### 1. **`test_package()` Function Signature and Return Type**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/cargo_build.rs` (lines 352-361)

```rust
/// Test a package with a spec.
pub fn test_package(
    pkg_name: &str,
    tspec: Option<&str>,
    cli_profile: Option<&str>,
    flags: &CargoFlags,
) -> Result<()> {
    run_cargo(CargoMode::Test, pkg_name, tspec, cli_profile, flags)?;
    Ok(())
}
```

**Key characteristics:**
- Returns `Result<()>` (success with no data, or error message)
- Delegates to `run_cargo()` with `CargoMode::Test`
- Does not capture test results/statistics—only passes/fails the entire operation
- No structured output is returned

---

### 2. **How Test Output Currently Flows**

**Stream-to-stdout, NOT captured:**

The `run_cargo()` function (lines 138-340 in `cargo_build.rs`) uses:
```rust
cmd.status()  // Returns ExitStatus, NOT captured output
```

This means:
- Test output goes **directly to the terminal** (streamed to stdout/stderr)
- Only the **exit status** is checked (`status.success()`)
- **No output is captured or parsed** in the main test flow

**Exception: `--list` and `--target-names` flags**

The `test.rs` command has special handling for `--list` and `--target-names`:
```rust
let output = cmd.output()  // Captures full output
let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
```

These parse:
- stderr for headers: `"Running ..."` and `"Doc-tests ..."` lines (lines 282-295)
- stdout for test lists: individual test names grouped by `"N tests, M benchmarks"` summary lines (lines 300-306)

---

### 3. **The `OpResult` Struct and Test Summaries**

**File:** `/home/wink/data/prgs/rust/tspec/src/all.rs` (lines 84-91)

```rust
/// Result of a batch operation on a single package
pub struct OpResult {
    pub name: String,
    pub spec: String,
    pub success: bool,
    pub message: String,
    pub size: Option<u64>,
}
```

**How it's used for tests:**

In `test_all()` (lines 252-314):
```rust
let result = match test_package(&member.name, tspec.as_deref(), cli_profile, flags) {
    Ok(()) => OpResult {
        name: member.name.clone(),
        spec,
        success: true,
        message: "ok".to_string(),  // Just "ok" — no parsed test data
        size: None,
    },
    Err(e) => OpResult {
        name: member.name.clone(),
        spec,
        success: false,
        message: e.to_string(),  // The error message
        size: None,
    },
};
```

**Summary printing** (`print_test_summary()`, lines 391-427):
```rust
let rows: Vec<SummaryRow> = results
    .iter()
    .map(|r| {
        let detail = if r.success {
            passed += 1;
            "[PASS]".to_string()
        } else {
            failed += 1;
            "[FAIL]".to_string()
        };
        // ...
    })
    .collect();

// Output: Package | Spec | Status
// Footer: "Test: 3 passed, 1 failed"
```

---

### 4. **Existing Output Parsing Code**

**Two distinct parsing implementations exist:**

1. **For `--list` / `--target-names`** (special test infrastructure commands):
   - Parses stderr for test target headers
   - Parses stdout for individual test names
   - Used to display available tests to users

2. **For actual test execution** (main `test` command):
   - **No parsing** of cargo test output
   - Only checks exit status
   - Output flows directly to terminal
   - Results summarized as binary pass/fail at the package level

---

### 5. **Subprocess Output Handling Details**

**Main execution path uses `status()`:**
```rust
cmd.status()  // Returns only ExitStatus, no captured output
```

**Consequence:**
- stdout/stderr are NOT captured
- Test output appears live in the terminal
- No programmatic access to test results (pass count, fail count, test names, etc.)

**Only `list_target_names()` and `list_tests()` use `output()`:**
```rust
let output = cmd.output()  // Captures stdout + stderr
let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
```

---

## Summary Table

| Aspect | Current Implementation |
|--------|------------------------|
| `test_package()` return type | `Result<()>` — binary pass/fail only |
| Test output streaming | **Streamed directly to terminal** via `cmd.status()` |
| Output parsing in main flow | **None** — no test results captured |
| OpResult for tests | `success: bool` + generic `message: String` (just "ok" or error) |
| Subprocess handling | `status()` for execution, `output()` only for `--list` helpers |
| Test summary granularity | Per-package pass/fail count only |

**Key Insight:** tspec currently has **no structured parsing of cargo test output**. This creates an opportunity to capture detailed test results (individual test passes/fails, timings, panic information) while maintaining backward compatibility with streaming output for live feedback.

    17→    #[arg(value_name = "PACKAGE")]
