    82→        }
    83→
    84→        if self.list {
    85→            return list_tests(resolved.as_deref(), &self.name_filter, flags);
    86→        }
    87→
    88→        // Build extra_args from --names, --test, and trailing args
    89→        let has_extra = !self.name_filter.is_empty()
    90→            || !self.test_target.is_empty()
    91→            || !self.test_args.is_empty();
    92→        let flags = if has_extra {
    93→            let mut f = flags.clone();
    94→            for target in &self.test_target {
    95→                f.extra_args.push("--test".to_string());
    96→                f.extra_args.push(target.clone());
    97→            }
    98→            // Name filters and test_args both go after --
    99→            // The test harness accepts [FILTERS...] as OR-matched substrings
   100→            if !self.name_filter.is_empty() || !self.test_args.is_empty() {
   101→                f.extra_args.push("--".to_string());
   102→                f.extra_args.extend(self.name_filter.clone());
   103→                f.extra_args.extend(self.test_args.clone());
   104→            }
   105→            f
   106→        } else {
   107→            flags.clone()
   108→        };
   109→
   110→        match resolved {
   111→            None => {
   112→                let workspace = WorkspaceInfo::discover()?;
   113→
   114→                // When --test targets are specified in workspace mode, only test
   115→                // packages that actually have matching files in tests/
   116→                if !self.test_target.is_empty() {
   117→                    let members = workspace.buildable_members();
   118→                    let matching: Vec<_> = members
   119→                        .iter()
   120→                        .filter(|m| {
   121→                            self.test_target
   122→                                .iter()
   123→                                .any(|t| m.path.join("tests").join(format!("{t}.rs")).exists())
   124→                        })
   125→                        .collect();
   126→
   127→                    if matching.is_empty() {
   128→                        eprintln!(
   129→                            "No packages contain test targets: {}",
   130→                            self.test_target.join(", ")
   131→                        );
   132→                        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   133→                    }
   134→
   135→                    for member in &matching {
   136→                        println!("=== {} ===", member.name);
   137→                        test_package(&member.name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   138→                    }
   139→                    return Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS);
   140→                }
   141→
   142→                let results =
   143→                    test_all(&workspace, &self.tspec, cli_profile, self.fail_fast, &flags);
   144→                Ok(print_test_summary(workspace.name(), &results))
   145→            }
   146→            Some(name) => {
   147→                if self.tspec.is_empty() {
   148→                    test_package(&name, None, cli_profile, &flags)?;
   149→                } else {
   150→                    let package_dir = resolve_package_dir(project_root, Some(&name))?;
   151→                    let pkg_name = get_package_name(&package_dir)?;
   152→                    let spec_paths = find_tspecs(&package_dir, &self.tspec)?;
   153→                    for spec_path in &spec_paths {
   154→                        let spec_str = spec_path.to_string_lossy();
   155→                        test_package(&pkg_name, Some(&spec_str), cli_profile, &flags)?;
   156→                    }
   157→                }
   158→                Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   159→            }
   160→        }
   161→    }
   162→}
   163→
   164→/// Format a cargo test target header into a human-friendly label.
   165→///
   166→/// Cargo emits headers like:
   167→/// - `unittests src/lib.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec-abc123)` → unit tests (no --test name)
   168→/// - `tests/tspec_test.rs (target/debug/deps/tspec_test-abc123)` → integration test
   169→///
   170→/// For integration tests, we show the `--test` usable name prominently.
   171→fn format_target_header(raw: &str) -> String {
   172→    // Strip the "(target/...)" suffix for cleaner output
   173→    let label = if let Some(idx) = raw.find(" (") {
   174→        &raw[..idx]
   175→    } else {
   176→        raw
   177→    };
   178→
   179→    if label.starts_with("unittests ") {
   180→        // "unittests src/lib.rs" → unit tests, not selectable with --test
   181→        format!("unit tests ({})", label.strip_prefix("unittests ").unwrap())
   182→    } else if label.starts_with("tests/") {
   183→        // "tests/tspec_test.rs" → extract basename for --test
   184→        let basename = label
   185→            .strip_prefix("tests/")
   186→            .and_then(|s| s.strip_suffix(".rs"))
   187→            .unwrap_or(label);
   188→        format!("{} (--test {})", label, basename)
   189→    } else {
   190→        label.to_string()
   191→    }
   192→}
   193→
   194→/// List available target names for `--test`.
   195→///
   196→/// Runs `cargo test --no-run` and parses stderr for "Running tests/..." lines,
   197→/// extracting the basename (without .rs) as the target name.
   198→fn list_target_names(package: Option<&str>, flags: &CargoFlags) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   199→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   200→
   201→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   202→    cmd.arg("test").arg("--no-run");
   203→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   204→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   205→    }
   206→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   207→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   208→
   209→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test --no-run")?;
   210→
   211→    if !output.status.success() {
   212→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   213→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   214→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   215→    }
   216→
   217→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   218→    let mut found = false;
   219→
   220→    println!("Available target names for --test:");
   221→    for line in stderr.lines() {
   222→        let trimmed = line.trim();
   223→        // --no-run emits "Executable tests/foo.rs (target/...)" for integration tests
   224→        if let Some(rest) = trimmed
   225→            .strip_prefix("Executable tests/")
   226→            .or_else(|| trimmed.strip_prefix("Running tests/"))
   227→        {
   228→            let file = if let Some(idx) = rest.find(" (") {
   229→                &rest[..idx]
   230→            } else {
   231→                rest
   232→            };
   233→            if let Some(name) = file.strip_suffix(".rs") {
   234→                println!("  {}    (tests/{})", name, file);
   235→                found = true;
   236→            }
   237→        }
   238→    }
   239→
   240→    if !found {
   241→        println!("  (none — no integration test files in tests/)");
   242→    }
   243→
   244→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   245→}
   246→
   247→/// Run `cargo test -- --list` and format the output.
   248→///
   249→/// Groups test functions under their target headers, showing counts per target
   250→/// and a total. Skips targets with zero tests.
   251→fn list_tests(
   252→    package: Option<&str>,
   253→    name_filter: &[String],
   254→    flags: &CargoFlags,
   255→) -> Result<ExitCode> {
   256→    let workspace = find_project_root()?;
   257→
   258→    let mut cmd = std::process::Command::new("cargo");
   259→    cmd.arg("test");
   260→    if let Some(pkg) = package {
   261→        cmd.arg("-p").arg(pkg);
   262→    }
   263→    flags.apply_to_command(&mut cmd);
   264→    cmd.arg("--");
   265→    cmd.args(name_filter);
   266→    cmd.arg("--list");
   267→    cmd.current_dir(&workspace);
   268→
   269→    let output = cmd.output().context("failed to run cargo test -- --list")?;
   270→
   271→    if !output.status.success() {
   272→        let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   273→        eprintln!("{}", stderr);
   274→        return Ok(ExitCode::from(1));
   275→    }
   276→
   277→    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);
   278→    let stdout = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stdout);
   279→
   280→    // Parse target headers from stderr ("Running ..." and "Doc-tests ..." lines)
   281→    // Produce human-friendly labels that show the --test name when applicable.
   282→    let targets: Vec<String> = stderr
   283→        .lines()
   284→        .filter_map(|line| {
   285→            let trimmed = line.trim();
   286→            if trimmed.starts_with("Running ") {
   287→                let rest = trimmed.strip_prefix("Running ").unwrap();
   288→                Some(format_target_header(rest))
   289→            } else if trimmed.starts_with("Doc-tests ") {
   290→                Some(trimmed.to_string())
   291→            } else {
   292→                None
   293→            }
   294→        })
   295→        .collect();
   296→
   297→    // Parse stdout into groups split by "N tests, M benchmarks" summary lines
   298→    let mut groups: Vec<Vec<&str>> = Vec::new();
   299→    let mut current: Vec<&str> = Vec::new();
   300→    for line in stdout.lines() {
   301→        if line.contains("tests,") && line.contains("benchmarks") {
   302→            groups.push(std::mem::take(&mut current));
   303→        } else if line.ends_with(": test") || line.ends_with(": bench") {
   304→            current.push(line);
   305→        }
   306→    }
   307→
   308→    // Print grouped output
   309→    let mut total = 0;
   310→    for (i, tests) in groups.iter().enumerate() {
   311→        if tests.is_empty() {
   312→            continue;
   313→        }
   314→        let unknown = "unknown target".to_string();
   315→        let header = targets.get(i).unwrap_or(&unknown);
   316→        println!("{} ({} tests)", header, tests.len());
   317→        for test in tests {
   318→            // Strip ": test" / ": bench" suffix for cleaner output
   319→            let name = test
   320→                .strip_suffix(": test")
   321→                .or_else(|| test.strip_suffix(": bench"))
   322→                .unwrap_or(test);
   323→            println!("  {}", name);
   324→        }
   325→        total += tests.len();
   326→        println!();
   327→    }
   328→
   329→    println!("{} tests total", total);
   330→    Ok(ExitCode::SUCCESS)
   331→}

Now I have a clear picture. The key challenge is that `run_cargo` uses `cmd.status()` for live streaming, but we need to capture output to parse test counts. Let me think about the approach.

The cleanest approach: change `run_cargo` (for Test mode) to use piped stdout/stderr, read lines in a loop, print them live AND accumulate them, then parse the `test result:` lines at the end. This gives both live output and parsed counts.

Let me also check what `test result:` lines look like from cargo:

Cargo outputs lines like:
```
test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out; finished in 0.02s
```

Now let me write the plan.

     1→# Plan: Remove classify_package and PackageKind
     2→
     3→## Context
     4→
     5→`classify_package()` hardcodes directory conventions (`apps/`, `libs/`, `tools/`, `*/tests`)
     6→that are specific to one workspace layout. tspec shouldn't impose layout opinions — cargo
     7→metadata already provides `has_binary`, and the only real behavioral need is excluding build
     8→tools (tspec/xt/xtask) from batch operations.
     9→
    10→## Changes
    11→
    12→### `src/workspace.rs`
    13→
    14→- **Remove** `PackageKind` enum (5 variants)
    15→- **Remove** `classify_package()` function
    16→- **Remove** `test_members()` method
    17→- **Replace** `kind: PackageKind` with `is_build_tool: bool` on `PackageMember`
    18→- **Add** `fn is_build_tool_name(name: &str) -> bool` — checks tspec/xt/xtask
    19→- **Update** `discover()` — set `is_build_tool` instead of calling `classify_package`
    20→- **Update** `buildable_members()` — filter `!m.is_build_tool`
    21→- **Update** `runnable_members()` — filter `m.has_binary && !m.is_build_tool`
    22→- **Replace unit tests** — drop all `classify_*` tests, add `is_build_tool_name` tests,
    23→  update `discover_works` to check `is_build_tool` instead of `PackageKind`
    24→
    25→### `src/all.rs`
    26→
    27→- **Remove** `use crate::workspace::PackageKind` import
    28→- **Remove** `use std::os::unix::fs::PermissionsExt` import
    29→- **Remove** `use crate::run::run_binary` import

    31→- **Remove** entire "Handle test packages" block in `test_all()` (test-binary-discovery)

{
  "file_path": "/home/wink/.claude/plans/stateful-finding-waffle.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Parse cargo test output for counts and filtering\n\n## Context\n\n`tspec test` currently streams cargo output live via `cmd.status()` and\nonly knows pass/fail per package. The summary shows `[PASS]`/`[FAIL]`\nwith no test counts. Worse, 0 tests running is reported as success.\n\nGoals:\n1. Parse `test result:` lines to extract passed/failed/ignored counts\n2. Show per-package test counts in the summary table\n3. Warn when 0 tests ran (likely a filter typo)\n4. Filter \"running 0 tests\" noise from single-target execution\n\n## Approach: Tee stdout with line parsing\n\nChange `run_cargo` (Test mode only) to pipe stdout, read lines in a\nloop, print each line live, and accumulate `test result:` lines. Return\nparsed counts alongside the exit status.\n\n### New struct: `TestResult`\n\n```rust\n/// Parsed test statistics from cargo test output\npub struct TestResult {\n    pub passed: u32,\n    pub failed: u32,\n    pub ignored: u32,\n    pub filtered: u32,\n}\n```\n\n### Changes\n\n#### `src/cargo_build.rs`\n\n- Add `TestResult` struct\n- Add `fn parse_test_result_line(line: &str) -> Option<TestResult>` —\n  parses `test result: ok. 263 passed; 0 failed; 0 ignored; 0 measured; 0 filtered out`\n- Change `test_package()` return type: `Result<()>` → `Result<TestResult>`\n- In `run_cargo()` for `CargoMode::Test`: use `cmd.stdout(Stdio::piped())`\n  and spawn, then read stdout line-by-line, printing each line AND\n  collecting `test result:` lines. Aggregate counts across all targets.\n  stderr stays inherited (compiler errors/warnings stream live).\n- Add unit tests for `parse_test_result_line`\n\n#### `src/all.rs`\n\n- Update `test_all()` to use new `TestResult` from `test_package()`\n- Store counts in `OpResult.message` or add fields to `OpResult`\n- Better: add `test_counts: Option<TestResult>` to `OpResult`\n\n#### `src/all.rs` — `print_test_summary()`\n\n- Show per-package counts: `[PASS]  263 passed` or `[FAIL]  2 failed`\n- Show totals in footer: `Test: 2 packages, 279 passed, 0 failed`\n- Warn when a package ran 0 tests:\n  `Warning: tspec-build ran 0 tests (filter may not match anything)`\n\n#### `src/cmd/test.rs`\n\n- Update single-package path to use `TestResult` return value\n- Warn on 0 tests for single-package execution too\n\n### Filtering \"running 0 tests\" noise\n\nWhile reading stdout line-by-line, suppress lines matching:\n- `running 0 tests` (when immediately followed by empty test result)\n- `test result: ok. 0 passed; 0 failed; ...` when all zeros\n\nOnly suppress when the block is entirely empty (0 passed, 0 failed).\nThis cleans up output when `--test` targets specific files.\n\n### What NOT to change\n\n- Build and run modes stay on `cmd.status()` (no parsing needed)\n- stderr stays inherited in all modes (compiler output streams live)\n- The `--list` and `--target-names` code paths are unaffected\n\n## Files to modify\n\n- `src/cargo_build.rs` — TestResult, parse function, test_package return type\n- `src/all.rs` — OpResult changes, test_all, print_test_summary\n- `src/cmd/test.rs` — handle TestResult in single-package path\n\n## Verification\n\n```\ntspec install --path .\ntspec test                    # Should show counts in summary\ntspec test -p tspec           # Should show counts\ntspec test -n nonexistent     # Should warn about 0 tests\ntspec clippy\ntspec fmt --check\n```\n"
}
